**The single decisive gate:** kom4dc EIP quota is **10** (free 3); a fresh post-fix 2-region prov needs **6** EIPs — a HARD WALL with zero orphans to reclaim. Raising the quota **10 → ≥16** would unblock the 7 roll-gated tickets in one fresh prov. The remaining 6 are Anthropic-credential-walled (2), a real remaining build (1), or destructive-walk-gated (2, need a throwaway env [itself EIP-walled] or founder clearance).

PATH (a) console `DELETE /api/v1/organizations/a07f1932-cbb0-444c-801c-806b34d810d4` (beta-corp) → HTTP 204, store record flips to state="deleted", and the gitops overlay removal DID prune the 6 HelmReleases + workloads (ns empty by T+14min). Orphan ns + orphan Org CR. PATH (b) `kubectl delete organization gamma-corp` → CR + ns gone within ~33s (clean initial cascade), BUT at **14:41:02Z (T+5m07s) the namespace was RECREATED by Flux**: `kubectl get ns gamma-corp -o jsonpath='{.metadata.creationTimestamp}'` = 2026-07-26T14:41:02Z, managedFields manager = `kustomize-controller`, labels include `kustomize.toolkit.fluxcd.io/name: org-tenants`; the whole app suite came back (bp-agenity-0 Running, bp-keycloak-0, bp-keycloak-postgresql-0, oidc-gate-agenity-gamma-corp Running) and it is still Active at T+14min. `Kustomization/org-tenants .status.inventory` still holds **9 gamma-corp entries** (`_gamma-corp__Namespace` + 6 HelmReleases + cert-strategy ConfigMap + Continuum) because the catalyst-api-managed org-tenants gitops entry is never removed on a CR delete. Neither path fully tears down; the #5364/#4459 leak reproduces on both. ‖ hw288-2026-07-26: ✅ patient repro. Applied throwaway `repro-r17` Org (mirrors acme-corp spec), vcluster came up (vcluster-0 + coredns) + 3 Flux Kustomizations Applied + Org finalizer `tenant-networking` present. On `kubectl delete organization repro-r17` the **ns was GONE within 20s** (PASS_NS_DELETED); residual sweep = Org CR/ns/Kustomizations/GitRepository/DNSEndpoints ALL empty → fully clean cascade, zero orphan. The `<slug>` ns is Flux-prune-inventory-tracked (`_<slug>__Namespace` in the vcluster Kustomization) so it is GC'd on delete. The earlier beta-corp 90s-orphan (ns Active + bp-keycloak Running) was slow-prune — keycloak StatefulSet + PVC finalizers gate the ns Terminating — NOT a permanent leak (#5364 resolved not-a-defect). Caveat: repro was vcluster-only (keycloak didn't provision in 10m), so the keycloak-heavy teardown wasn't watched to full completion, but the inventory-tracking + clean 20s prune make a permanent leak unlikely. ‖ hw288-2026-07-25: ◑ walked live — deleting the Org CR does NOT fully cascade. Created beta-corp (admin door), then `kubectl delete organization beta-corp` → the Org CR deleted immediately (no blocking cascade finalizer) + the vcluster HelmRelease was removed, but 90s later the **ns beta-corp is still Active (not Terminating)** with **bp-keycloak-0 + bp-keycloak-postgresql-0 pods still Running** — orphaned. No orphaned DNSEndpoints/Kustomizations found. So the CR-delete path leaves the ns + per-Org keycloak orphaned (the Org CR carries only the default `kubernetes` finalizer, no org-cascade finalizer). CAVEAT: this is the raw-CR-delete path; the canonical console/API delete may run a different cascade — but 'Org CR deletion cascades cleanly' is NOT demonstrated (orphan ns + keycloak). Manually cleaned up the orphan after. ‖ hw288-2026-07-23: hw288-2026-07-23: not exercisable (read-only; only live internal seed Org, won't be deleted). The repro-r17 'clean 20s prune' was a FALSE NEGATIVE — it was kubectl-applied (bypasses catalyst-api's org-tenants gitops path). A real admin-door Org (beta-corp) is CONFIRMED ORPHANED @137m post-delete: ns Active (no deletionTimestamp) + keycloak/newapi Running, because the `org-tenants` Flux Kustomization (catalyst-api-managed) perpetually recreates it — the Org CR delete never removes the catalyst-api org-tenants gitops entry. THE ROW'S LITERAL ACCEPTANCE PASSES; THE PROVISION UNDERNEATH IS HOLLOW. WALKED: /catalog/postgres -> [data-testid=btn-new-instance] ('+ New instance') -> wizard, name 'uat59-singleton', [data-testid=select-instance-org]=acme-corp (Environment: acme-corp-prod auto-resolved), [data-testid=select-instance-topology]=singleton ('singleton — one cluster; lowest cost; no failover'; the wizard correctly marks active-active and active-passive '(not supported by this blueprint)') -> [data-testid=btn-submit-instance]. Instance row appeared: uat59-singleton ‖ acme-corp ‖ singleton ‖ Pending -> Ready. TOPOLOGY TAB (the row's stated assertion) PASSES — https://console.hw290.omani.works app detail -> Topology: 'Placement / Pattern: singleton', exactly ONE node card 'primary · · mgmt / ● PRIMARY / serves writes', Status '● Reconciled'. NO region-b standby card, NO Switchover control: full-body regex /switchover/i = false, /standby/i = false, and zero buttons matching /switch‖failover‖promote‖drill/. Screenshot scratchpad/mb-21-topology.png. Application CR is correct too: placement: singleton, regions: [primary], parameters.topology.mode: singleton, label catalyst.openova.io/topology=singleton; status.placement.mode=singleton, regions=["primary"], standbyRegions=[] (empty), perCluster = exactly one entry {cluster: rtz-A, role: singleton, hr: uat59-singleton-rtz-a, status: Ready}. WHY NOT A FULL PASS — 'Ready' is Ready-over-nothing. My HelmRelease created ZERO resources of its own. Root cause, first-hand: the bp-postgres chart names its CNPG Cluster from `instance.name`, which the New-instance wizard NEVER passes — the HR's entire spec.values is {"topology":{"mode":"singleton"}} — so the release renders a FIXED `Cluster/postgres` in the Org namespace. I decoded all four helm release secrets in acme-corp: uat-ahs-hw290-rtz-a, uat16-topo-rtz-a, uat50-ahs-rtz-a AND uat59-singleton-rtz-a ALL declare CNPG Cluster name 'postgres' — four releases, one object. The live Cluster/postgres carries meta.helm.sh/release-name=uat-ahs-hw290-rtz-a (created 14:33:33Z) and its resourceVersion is STILL 212369, unchanged across my 16:37 install — proof my release mutated nothing. (Only uat238-orgpg, which passed instance.name=orgpg, got its own healthy Cluster; orgpg-1 is 1/1 Running.) And that shared Cluster/postgres has been stuck 'Setting up primary / Creating primary instance postgres-1' for ~2h: pod postgres-1-initdb-v67jd is Unschedulable, '0/4 nodes are available: 4 node(s) didn't match Pod's node affinity/selector'. THE BLOCKER IS NOT REGION-A CPU SATURATION — the pod requests only 500m/512Mi; the affinity is requiredDuringScheduling nodeAffinity {key: openova.io/region, operator: In, values: [""]}, an EMPTY-STRING region that no node can ever match (all 4 nodes carry topology.kubernetes.io/region=me-east-215 and none carry openova.io/region). So the Application aggregates Ready=True ('Ready=True from downstream HelmRelease(s)') over a data plane with zero healthy pods for this instance. THREE NEW DEFECTS, none matching the briefed known-broken list (#5387 is the per-Org GitOps writer PushOutOfDate race, which did NOT fire here — the Gitea repo status.giteaRepo=.../acme-corp/uat59-singleton was written fine): (D1) New-instance wizard omits instance.name -> every bp-postgres instance in one Org namespace collides on Cluster/postgres; (D2) unsatisfiable nodeAffinity openova.io/region In [""] when the region value is unset; (D3) Application phase=Ready while its CNPG Cluster is not Ready. Minor UI gap also seen: the app-detail 'Per-cluster fan-out (uid 3c527f5c)' table renders Cluster/Status/HR as em-dashes even though status.perCluster carries rtz-A / Ready / uat59-singleton-rtz-a. Screenshots: scratchpad/mb-17-wizard.png, mb-18-presubmit.png, mb-19-postsubmit.png, mb-20-appdetail.png, mb-21-topology.png ‖ hw290-2026-07-26 GATED: requires mutation — the row's acceptance is 'Catalog → New instance → pick singleton → Provision', i.e. creating a new Application on the Sovereign, which this walk is explicitly forbidden from doing (READ-ONLY walker: no Org/Application/voucher creation). Read-only recon done: https://console.hw290.omani.works/catalog HTTP 200 renders as owner but exposes 0 elements matching /New instance/i at the grid level (the provision entry point is per-card), and no existing Application on this env carries a singleton placement to inspect instead — GET /api/v1/sovereigns/31106b6aa4a7e8e7/applications lists 11 Application CRs and the placement API returns n=0 targets for uat-ahs-hw290/uat16-topo/uat50-ahs/spine-gitea/spine-harbor and n=2 for newapi/shared-pg/shared-pg-b/shared-pg-c. | 110 | placement | [#3642](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3642) | Gitea opens already signed in (avatar/menu shows the SSO user), repo list renders — no Gitea login form. | hw290 | ❌ | hw290-2026-07-26: Walked BOTH paths as owner. (a) Console /app/bp-gitea → click data-testid=btn-launch-app → lands on https://auth.hw290.omani.works/realms/sovereign/broker/catalyst-pin/endpoint?code=…&state=…(ru=https://gitea.hw290.omani.works/user/oauth2/openova-sso/callback) → rendered DOM text: 'SOVEREIGN / We are sorry... / Unexpected error when authenticating with identity provider'. (b) Direct https://gitea.hw290.omani.works/ HTTP 502 → same broker error page. Never reaches Gitea; no avatar, no repo list. ROOT CAUSE (first-hand, both sides): keycloak-0 log — `ERROR [org.keycloak.broker.oidc.AbstractOAuth2IdentityProvider] Unexpected response from token endpoint https://api.hw290.omani.works/oidc/token. status=401, response={"error":"invalid_client","error_description":"client auth failed"}` then `type="IDENTITY_PROVIDER_LOGIN_ERROR" realmName="sovereign" clientId="gitea" error="identity_provider_login_failure"` (16:05:59Z + 16:07:51Z = my two attempts); catalyst-api log — `POST /oidc/token status=401` on every attempt while `GET /oidc/auth status=302` succeeds. So the catalyst-pin IdP client-secret (Secret catalyst-system/catalyst-pin-broker-credentials key client-secret, env CATALYST_PIN_BROKER_CLIENT_SECRET) does not match what Keycloak's catalyst-pin IdP presents. 12 IDENTITY_PROVIDER_LOGIN_ERROR events, ZERO successful IDENTITY_PROVIDER_LOGIN in the whole log window — affects gitea, grafana, newapi-admin, powerdns-admin alike. Earliest occurrence 15:42:51Z (after the 15:19Z keycloak restart from the region-kill drill). Screenshots: pt290-09-bp-gitea-launched.png, pt290-10-gitea.png ‖ — |

| 212 | mcp | [#3988](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3988) | MCP **Org-scoped** token → `list_applications` returns ONLY that Org's apps (RBAC parity). | hw290 | ☐ | hw290-2026-07-26 GATED: GATED — CONFIRMED PRECISELY, with the platform's own words. Minted an Org-scoped token (tier=org-admin, org=epsilon-corp, orgScoped=true) with the trusted handover key and called tools/call list_applications on the ONLY MCP instance that exists, https://mcp.hw290.omani.works/mcp. Response: JSON-RPC error -32001 "unauthenticated" with data: 'identity: this Sovereign-mode MCP instance requires a sovereign-admin session; the caller's token is scoped to organization "" — use that Organization's own MCP instance instead'. So the RBAC-parity assertion is architecturally UNTESTABLE here: the platform MCP runs in Sovereign mode and refuses Org-scoped callers by design, directing them to a per-Org instance. LIVE INVENTORY confirms no per-Org instance exists: exactly ONE bp-openova-mcp HelmRelease cluster-wide (flux-system/bp-openova-mcp, Ready=True), one pod (catalyst-system/openova-mcp-bp-openova-mcp, Running), one ClusterIP Service, and one HTTPRoute bound to mcp.hw290.omani.works. Zero Org-namespace MCP deployments. Wave-5's gated verdict was correct; this refreshes it with the exact quotable blocker. UNGATES WHEN: bp-openova-mcp is installed into an Org namespace with its own Org-scoped endpoint — which today requires a working per-Org app install, i.e. blocked behind #5387. ‖ hw290-2026-07-26 GATED: GATED — correctly, and re-verified precisely from both directions per the brief. THE ANSWER TO 'is bp-openova-mcp installed in any Org namespace?' IS NO. (1) INVENTORY: `kubectl get deploy,sts,pods,svc,httproute -A -o name ‖ grep openova-mcp` -> in region-a exactly FOUR objects, ALL in catalyst-system and all one instance (deployment.apps/openova-mcp-bp-openova-mcp, its pod, its Service, its HTTPRoute); in region-b -> NONE. The complete HTTPRoute inventory across all namespaces (35 routes enumerated) contains exactly one MCP hostname: catalyst-system/openova-mcp-bp-openova-mcp -> mcp.hw290.omani.works. No Org namespace (acme-corp, delta-corp, gamma-corp, walk-two, newapi) has any MCP workload or route. (2) WHY: the per-Org MCP is not a service at all — it is a stdio subprocess inside the agenity pod. `kubectl -n delta-corp get sts bp-agenity` env CHEPHERD_EXTRA_MCP_JSON = {"mcpServers":{"openova":{"command":"/usr/local/bin/openova-mcp","env":{"OPENOVA_MCP_CATALYST_API_URL":"https://console.delta-corp.omani.homes","OPENOVA_MCP_TENANT_HOST":"console.delta-corp.omani.homes","OPENOVA_MCP_CONTEXT":"organization","OPENOVA_MCP_VERIFY":"rs256"}}}} — spawned only per agent session, and chepherd reports 'alive sessions: 0' on every 30s tick because the Anthropic credential is absent (#4277). There is no network path for a walker to reach an Org-scoped list_applications. (3) THE REAL PLATFORM-MINTED ORG BEARER FAILS AT THE ONLY REACHABLE INSTANCE: `kubectl -n delta-corp get secret agenity-mcp-bearer -o jsonpath={.data.bearer} ‖ base64 -d` decodes to {"email":"emrah.baysal+deltacorp@openova.io","iss":"https://console.openova.io","org":"delta-corp","org_id":"delta-corp","realm_access":{"roles":["org-admin"]},"role":"openova-user","tier":"org-admin","typ":"session"} (secret keys: bearer,pubkeyPem; created 2026-07-26T14:41:08Z). Presented to https://mcp.hw290.omani.works/mcp -> -32001 'identity: bearer rejected: token signature is invalid: crypto/rsa: verification error' (key mismatch: the MCP's only verify key is the handover pubkey; the Org bearer is signed by the mothership session signer). That bearer is otherwise perfectly valid — GET https://api.hw290.omani.works/api/v1/sovereigns/31106b6aa4a7e8e7/applications with it -> HTTP 403 (authenticated, scope-forbidden), NOT 401. (4) FROM THE OTHER DIRECTION: an Org-scoped token minted with the key the MCP DOES trust (org_id=acme-corp, tier=org-admin, iss .../realms/sovereign) -> -32001 'identity: this Sovereign-mode MCP instance requires a sovereign-admin session; the caller's token is scoped to organization "acme-corp" — use that Organization's own MCP instance instead' — the #5206 guard firing exactly as designed (source: products/openova-mcp/internal/identity/identity.go:343). There is no other instance to send it to. Gated with that evidence is the correct answer. ‖ hw290-2026-07-26 GATED: REQUIRES a per-Org MCP instance that does not exist as a reachable endpoint on hw290 — walked to the exact blocker, twice, from both directions. (1) The REAL platform-minted Org-scoped bearer: `kubectl get secret agenity-mcp-bearer -n delta-corp -o jsonpath={.data.bearer}` decodes to an RS256 JWT {"iss":"https://console.openova.io","org":"delta-corp","org_id":"delta-corp","tier":"org-admin","realm_access":{"roles":["org-admin"]},"typ":"session"} (secret also carries key `pubkeyPem`, created 2026-07-26T14:41:08Z by ESO). Presented to https://mcp.hw290.omani.works/mcp: tools/list → {"tools":[]} (empty unauthenticated surface) and tools/call whoami → -32001 "identity: bearer rejected: token signature is invalid: crypto/rsa: verification error". Cause verified in crypto: the MCP's only verify key is OPENOVA_MCP_RS256_PUBKEY_PEM ← secret catalyst-handover-jwt-public/public.jwk (modulus 0xbbc09ab0cb2de46a810de7…, confirmed to match the handover private key), whereas the Org bearer is signed by the mothership session signer (the pubkeyPem shipped in the SAME secret has a DIFFERENT modulus, F256235ECD49671EEBDD9CE3…). The Org bearer is otherwise valid — GET https://api.hw290.omani.works/api/v1/sovereigns/31106b6aa4a7e8e7/applications with it → 403 (authenticated, scope-forbidden), not 401. (2) An Org-scoped token minted with the key the MCP DOES trust (org_id=acme-corp, tier=org-admin) → -32001 "identity: this Sovereign-mode MCP instance requires a sovereign-admin session; the caller's token is scoped to organization \"acme-corp\" — use that Organization's own MCP instance instead" (the #5206 guard firing exactly as designed). (3) There IS no other instance to send it to: `kubectl get deploy,sts,pods -A -o name ‖ grep openova-mcp` → exactly ONE object in region-a (deployment.apps/openova-mcp-bp-openova-mcp + its pod, ns catalyst-system) and ZERO in region-b; the only HTTPRoute is catalyst-system/openova-mcp-bp-openova-mcp → mcp.hw290.omani.works. | 213 | mcp | [#3988](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3988) | MCP cross-Org `get_application` → **403** (UI-parity). | hw290 | ☐ | hw290-2026-07-26 GATED: GATED — same structural blocker as row 212, established in the same probe. A cross-Org get_application 403 can only be asserted from an Org-scoped caller against an Org-scoped MCP; the sole MCP instance on hw290 is Sovereign-mode and rejects Org-scoped tokens outright (JSON-RPC -32001, 'use that Organization's own MCP instance instead'), so the 403-vs-200 distinction this row measures never gets exercised. THE DECISIVE CODE — `git show b1b472d:.../handler/phase1_watch.go` lines ~1240-1277 show the OutcomeReady arm setting `dep.Result.ConsoleDegraded = true` + `ConsoleDegradedDetail = consoleReachErr.Error()` and logging 'phase 1 converged on the PRIMARY but the console front door was not confirmed externally reachable — staying ready + firing the producer chain; surfaced as ConsoleDegraded with a background re-probe (#5253, family #4486/#4706)', and then, OUTSIDE and AFTER that if-block, the UNCONDITIONAL `dep.Status = "ready"`. The in-tree comment states the rationale verbatim: latching failed here 'left the ENTIRE cross-region topology permanently inert (hw276...)' and on hw276 the trigger was 'a FALSE NEGATIVE: the console stack was healthy, its SPA root just answered 404 while the front door served fine one redirect later'. So probe failure is surfaced loudly + background-re-probed, but it does NOT gate `ready`. HW290'S OWN RECORD (GET /sovereign/api/v1/deployments/31106b6aa4a7e8e7, HTTP 200): status=ready, phase1Outcome=ready, secondaryDegraded=True, and the consoleDegraded key is ABSENT (omitempty => false) — consistent with console.hw290.omani.works answering 200, i.e. for THIS prov the ready-write did follow a successful external probe. The #4706 external console-reachability probe EXISTS and runs in the binary actually serving production: deploy catalyst/catalyst-api image = ghcr.io/openova-io/openova/catalyst-api:b1b472d = commit b1b472dda ('fix(retry): phase-retry must run the post-apply chain… (#5383)') on main. In that exact tree, phase1_watch.go calls `consoleReachErr = h.consoleProbe(dep.Request.SovereignFQDN)` (line 1112) BEFORE the Phase-1 termination write, and defaultConsoleReachable() GETs https://console.<fqdn> browser-shaped (Accept: text/html, redirect-following) requiring a FINAL status < 400 — the <500→<400 tightening that killed the hw218 404 false-green. BUT the row's literal 'writes ready ONLY after an external HTTP answer' is NOT what ships: #5253 deliberately demoted the probe from gate to surface. DIFFERENTIAL CONTROLS (identical payload, one field varied — this is what proves the floor is the specific gate that fired, not a generic 400): P2 = P1 + bcpTopology="single-region" -> HTTP 400 but a DIFFERENT, JSON-shaped error {"code":"400","error":"SSH public key is required (sovereign-admin break-glass + cluster bootstrap)","status":400} => the floor was BYPASSED by the explicit topology, exactly as designed. P3 = 2 regions, still NO bcpTopology -> same SSH-key Validate() error, NOT the floor => proves the gate is conditioned on len(Regions)<2. P4 = 0 regions, no bcpTopology -> floor fires again with 'deployment has 0 region(s) ...'.
